# archaeo-stats-in-R

## simple statistics in R for archaeologists
- ran first cell, installed packages - first time it stalled for five minutes, so I restarted it and it completed without error within 55s
- ran second cell, "told R" to use three specific packages, that being "car", "carData" and "sandwich" - no errors

## 1. Initial exploration for numerical and categorical data
- ran first cell, brought up the "DartPoints dataframe" showing a table with several columns and rows - no errors
- ran second cell, calculated the average length of the dart points in a specific column using three different methods of producing the same result - no errors
- ran third cell, summarized the "Length" variable of the "DartPoints" dataframe - no errors
- ran fourth cell, specified the amount of digits to show, then summarized all the columns from 5 to 11 - no errors
- edited fifth cell, replicated the code from the fourth cell but changed the positions from 5:11 to 1:11 to include columns 1 through 4, ran it - received the following error:
  - " Error in colMeans(X, na.rm = TRUE): 'x' must be numeric or complex
      Traceback:
      
      1. colMeans(X, na.rm = TRUE)
      2. .handleSimpleError(function (cnd) 
       . {
       .     watcher$capture_plot_and_output()
       .     cnd <- sanitize_call(cnd)
       .     watcher$push(cnd)
       .     switch(on_error, continue = invokeRestart("eval_continue"), 
       .         stop = invokeRestart("eval_stop"), error = NULL)
       . }, "'x' must be numeric or complex", base::quote(colMeans(X, 
       .     na.rm = TRUE)))"
    - I believe this is because the columns 1 to 4, unlike 5 to 11, do not contain raw numbers, so the "numSummary" command cannot compute them
- ran sixth cell, defined the variable DP_Type by the command "table(DartPoints$Name)" which gets outputs how many points each name has, and received the output below; then found the proportion of each name by using the prop.table command on the DP_Type variable and multiplying by one-hundred, which outputted successfully the proportions of each name - no errors

### Initial exploration for categorical data
- ran first cell, defined the variable DP_CT by the resulting table of each name and their respective numbers of each blade shape - no errors
- ran second cell, used the addmargins command with the aforedefined DP_CT variable to sum all the blade shapes for each name - no errors
- ran third cell, used the dim command on DartPoints to give the dimension of "91 - 17", which I learned from the next cell that this specifically means 91 rows and 71 columns, even though the total sum from the previous cell showed 89 - no errors
- ran fourth cell, used the View command to see all of DartPoints as a table - no errors; some of the data is missing but the next cell explains why
- ran fifth cell, used addmargins command with the same xtabs command from the first cell to add the missing data, now the sum accurately reported 91 - no errors

- edited second cell, used the summary command with BACups$H to give me a summary of the total height of all the cups; ran it - no errors:
  - "Min. 1st Qu.  Median    Mean 3rd Qu.    Max. 
    3.30    4.95    6.30    6.99    8.85   14.30"
- edited third cell, tried to define the variable Protoapennine by only the rows that had the Phase value of "Protoapennine" using the command "(Protoapennine <- (BACups$Phase = Protoapennine))"; I was trying to replicate the earlier command "(DP_Type <- table(DartPoints$Name))" but with a specific value for the chosen column rather than all of its values - error:
  -  "Error: object 'Protoapennine' not found
      Traceback:"
  - I searched up some R documentation and saw this website showing the operators of R: https://www.w3schools.com/r/r_operators.asp
    - from there I realized that I used "=" instead of "==", where the former defines a variable and the latter compares two variables to see if they are identical; so I edited my command to be "(Protoapennine <- (BACups$Phase == Protoapennine))" - gave me the same exact error
  - I looked around some more and found this website explaining the different data types in R: https://www.r-bloggers.com/2023/09/understanding-data-types-in-r/
    - from there I learned that my use of Protoapennine in the right-hand side of the command was an object, since it had no quotations around it, but what I really wanted was a Character String, because that is the type of value that the Phase column uses in BACups, which uses quotations
    - thus, I edited my command to be "(Protoapennine <- (BACups$Phase == "Protoapennine"))" and ran it; it outputted a series of FALSE FALSE FALSE... TRUE TRUE TRUE because I included the command in brackets - no errors
      - the output was a list of FALSE FALSE FALSE ... TRUE TRUE TRUE ... statements, which was basically just checking each row and telling me if it was TRUE or FALSE that the Phase column contained the "Protoapennine" string
      - I write "no errors" here because, even though that was not my intended effect, the command still ran cleanly
  - since my only programming experience is from Grade 11 Computer Science, I found it my lack of documentation literacy frustrating and resorted to just pasting my command in Google and writing "how to make this isolate for only the rows with Protoapeninne rather than just showing if each row has it or not"
    - the Google AI gave me an answer - I am generally hesitant to use this, but I didn't even know the language to use to search up a problem to my solution, and in my frustration, the AI did show me a simple answer that worked; I do apologize if this is antagonistic to the learning I am supposed to be demonstrating, but I do think that it helped me learn in this case: I learned from it that the normal parentheses () are for functions and the square brackets [] are for selecting data from an object, which makes the earlier cells easier to understand as well
  - with my new knowledge of the square brackets, I edited it to be "Protoapennine <- \[BACups$Phase == "Protoapennine"]" - error:
    - " Error in parse(text = input): <text>:1:18: unexpected '['
      1: Protoapennine <- [
                     ^
      Traceback:"
    - I looked at the earlier commands with DartPoints and realized that I forgot to place an object before the brackets for the data to be selected from
  - thus, I changed it to: "Protoapennine <- BACups\[BACups$Phase == "Protoapennine"]" - error:
    - " Error in `[.data.frame`(BACups, BACups$Phase == "Protoapennine"): undefined columns selected
        Traceback:
        
        1. `[.data.frame`(BACups, BACups$Phase == "Protoapennine")
        2. stop("undefined columns selected")
        3. .handleSimpleError(function (cnd) 
         . {
         .     watcher$capture_plot_and_output()
         .     cnd <- sanitize_call(cnd)
         .     watcher$push(cnd)
         .     switch(on_error, continue = invokeRestart("eval_continue"), 
         .         stop = invokeRestart("eval_stop"), error = NULL)
         . }, "undefined columns selected", base::quote(`[.data.frame`(BACups, 
         .     BACups$Phase == "Protoapennine")))"
    - looking back again! I realized that I forgot to include the comma, because I think it's always checking for two things (I'm not sure what) even when you're not using it
  - thus, I changed it to: "Protoapennine <- BACups\[BACups$Phase == "Protoapennine", ]" - ran without error, hurrah!

## 2. Graphs
- ran first cell, loaded and viewed the Fibulae data set - no errors
- ran second cell, defined "Fib_coils" by a table of the Coils column in the Fibulae set - no errors
- ran third cell, created a pie chart by calling the Fib_coils variable, titling it "La Tene Bronze Fibulae", titling the bottom part "Number of Coils" and setting Clockwise= to be TRUE - no errors
- ran fourth cell, created a barplot using mostly the same variables from the third cell but with the ylab variable instead of Clockwise=, which was named "Number of Fibulae" - no errors
  - this one seems more useful because the pie graph only has one axis and no legend making it harder to understand than the bar plot with two axes
- ran fifth cell, loaded the PitHouses dataset - no errors
- ran sixth cell, defined the PitHouses.tbl variable as a "cross-tabulation" comparing the Hearths with Size using the xtabs command from earlier - no errors
- ran seventh cell, created a barplot using the PitHouses.tbl variable - no errors:
  - <img width="840" height="840" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/f70a6577-8687-46c6-af2a-353cab488fa9" />

## 3. Chi-squared test of association
- ran first cell, created a table comparing the Orient with Form - no errors
- ran second cell, used the Chi-square test to check how different the expected result is from the outputted result, and did not apply the Yates' correction - error:
  - " Warning message in chisq.test(PitHouses$Orient, PitHouses$Form, correct = FALSE):
    “Chi-squared approximation may be incorrect”
    
    	Pearson's Chi-squared test
    
    data:  PitHouses$Orient and PitHouses$Form
    X-squared = 0.007, df = 1, p-value = 0.9" 
- ran third cell, checked for a correlation between hearth number and form - no errors
- ran fourth cell, checked if the p value was such that we could accept or reject the null hypothesis - error:
  - " Warning message in chisq.test(PitHouses$Hearths, PitHouses$Form, correct = FALSE):
    “Chi-squared approximation may be incorrect”
    
    	Pearson's Chi-squared test
    
    data:  PitHouses$Hearths and PitHouses$Form
    X-squared = 2, df = 3, p-value = 0.5"
- ran fifth cell, loaded and viewed the EWBurials dataset - no errors
- ran sixth cell, used the Chi-squared test on the EWBurials comparing Goods with Group; the value p-value was 0.02, meaning we reject the null hypothesis - no errors
